# LAG2 · Do sadness and loneliness carry over more than anger across prompts?

**Registered prediction.** The next-prompt to same-prompt slope ratio should be larger for sadness and loneliness than for anger.

**Source and sample.** Class P. Public openESM diaries. Use the original equal-diary roster and add member 0057 following verified replay. Member 0049 has a [separate public report](LAG-0049.md). The table identifies contributing people or paired observations for each estimate.

**Registered quantities.** Select from the original same-prompt atlas. Retain its clock, source, seeds, two gap readings and whole-person intervals. First take the median within diary and then across contributing diaries. The table shows restored cells, identified support and family summaries. The old pool is independently reproduced before adding the newly verified diary.

Pooled PANIC/GRIEF, all, median ratio: 0.0490565 (2 diaries).

**Verdict — descriptive.** The two pooled gap readings disagree about the ordering of sadness and loneliness versus anger. The study did not register an interval for the difference between family medians.

**Limit.** An event may last across both prompts. Missed prompts can widen gaps selectively, and the two families may draw on different cells and diaries.

**Withholding and release condition.** All registered pooled readings and newly verified cell estimates are available. No interval was registered for the pooled ratio or the family difference.
